There are three motions for the opening of a parliamentary investigation, and I'm going to read it: Turkey to the chairman of the Grand National Assembly The issue of disability in the world has been put into universal norms, and democratic and social states have been burdened with important responsibilities in this area. The rights of our 8.5 million disabled citizens, which make up 12.29 per cent of the population in Turkey, are guaranteed by various laws and international contracts, especially our Constitution. It is now inevitable that there will be arrangements for the development of our disabled in all respects and full participation in social life and the co-ordination of these services, and the improvement of what has been done. Troubles with disabled persons are not only their own and their families' problems. These problems concern the entire community. The recovery of disabled people and relatives who feel health, education, employment, care, rehabilitation, accessibility, and many sociocultural and economic problems in the most burning way can be resolved by the awareness and even training of society in this field. It is a fact that there is no doubt that if our disabled citizens can fully use their human rights and basic freedoms, they can make positive contributions to society. This reality is sufficient reason to encourage our disabled citizens to join the community. This reality will also contribute to the human, social, and economic development of society and to the decline of poverty. It is important and priority to develop special social support programmes for disabled people with fewer opportunities if it is considered a basic priority to achieve a social justice covering all communities. In order for disabled people and their families to benefit equally from opportunities and services, they must be mobilized with all the power of the social state. It is the main duty of the social state to provide and promote vocational services for disabled persons, education and rehabilitation, employment and care and protection services. Determinate steps must be taken to improve the opportunities of our disabled citizens and to increase their access to all areas of social life. One of the obstacles to the integration of disabled people into society is the problems they face in education. The education system in all countries is primarily planned and implemented for the non-impaired part of the population. So from the very beginning, the educational system has an understanding that excludes disabled persons, and then there are various programmes that are designed to integrate disabled people with the education system. Therefore, education infrastructure must first be developed as quality and quantity and reach a level that meets the needs of the disabled. Another problem for our disabled citizens is that the physical environment is not designed according to our disabled people. When designing society, when putting together a model of society, it is necessary to design the physical environment that lives within it, thinking about everyone who lives in it. Roads, sidewalks, public buildings, parks and gardens, schools, dwellings, transportation vehicles, and many other physical environmental elements, such as this, are a serious obstacle to the participation of disabled people in society. Thus, because of its disability, the limitations of people who are limited to their ability to move are even greater. It means that the ability to act is the exclusion of the limited individual from social life. All of this, however, can be designed and developed in a way that will facilitate the integration of disabled people into society. Our disabled citizens demand a comprehensive investigation of health, education, employment, care, rehabilitation, accessibility, and solutions to many sociocultural and economic problems, the Constitution of 98, Internal Affairs 104 and 105. In accordance with the clauses, we'd like to submit the requirement for a parliamentary investigation. In fact, internal and external control is a much more detailed audit. Internal control is now a very fundamental element of the modern public administration, the public financial administration. Turkey passed through this system in 2000. We adopted such a law in this Parliament at the end of 2003 with the support of the Republican People's Party. Turkey won a modern system; internal control. There's also an outside audit, there's an institution. The internal audit of the institution makes its own internal auditors. This is an audit outside of income and expense control. In fact, instead of coming and spending control, perhaps it would be more appropriate to order internal control. Outside control is the control of an outside institution, an independent institution, other than its own members. But you have to write it clearly to the law.
